In short, I believe that we have made an effort to reach consensus, to agree, to reduce tension, to appeal to unity among all. ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u24	Mr. President of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u25	Thank you very much, Madam President. Thank you, Your Honor, for your question. Indeed, citizens deserve clarity and the truth, and therefore the debates that need to be raised in this House must be contextualised. The Government of Spain has proposed that the response to this crisis must be a positive, sustainable and inclusive response. That is the approach taken by the Spanish Government both in Europe and in our country. Now, if you're asking me if there's going to be cuts or there's going to be cuts, what I'm asking you is this, Your Honor. What has to be there are countercyclical policies that respond to the cycle in reverse. That is, when there is an economic crisis, what we have to do is to debt, to spend more, because we have to protect many sectors as we are doing, but, of course, situations with a deficit of 10% of gross domestic product will have to cut them in the future. And how do we have to cut it? Firstly, by spreading growth and, secondly, by reforming taxation so that there is greater fiscal justice than there has been now, and obviously there will be - let us hope - policies that will logically be reduced due to greater job creation. Consequently, we will not have to provide so much protection to unemployment because there will be a reduction in the unemployment rate in our country. In short, ladies and gentlemen, if we have to do that approach, that economic policy debate, we are open, but let us do it seriously, rigorously, and being very aware that this is a government committed to a positive response to the crisis from the point of view of social inclusion, as we have done, for example, with minimum vital income, or also from the point of view of sustainability, as we are doing during these last Councils of Ministers, with the adoption of draft laws as important as that of climate change, the circular economy strategy, or even yesterday with the adoption, for example, of a strengthening of CO2 emission markets, which is also something that commits our Government to this adaptation and to this mitigation of climate change. With this not encouraging scenario, I go up to this rostrum to explain different aspects related to railway communications, which are a basic factor of localization to overcome the isolation that penalizes competitiveness and to promote job creation. Asturias is at the head of rail incommunicado in Spain and Europe, as reflected in official indicators, hence the urgent need for the application of remedies to ensure that the improvement of quality, the renovation of equipment, the modernization of the train, instead of its dismantling, the completion of infrastructures that have been prolonging for many years and, ultimately, the agreement to comply with the specific agreements and plans committed are a reality within the shortest possible time. I would like to raise a number of questions for which I would like to ask your attention, Minister, in view of the fact that the state of alarm which has been in force since 14 March has not been stopped so that decisions have been announced on railway matters, with which the Socialists return to their actions to the detriment of the Asturians. I will begin by referring to an endless action, that of the underground in Langreo for the integration of the railway line Gijón-Laviana, works that began in 2009 after the project was included at the beginning of the century by the first government of the PP within the Plan of Coal Mining. It was Mr Rodríguez Zapatero who came to the Presidency and the work that was going to last thirty-six months began to go through many budgetary vicissitudes. They have been paralysed since 2017, waiting to sign a new agreement between the concerned administrations, the fourth in the series, which is key to be able to complete the execution of the project, which, in any case, will hardly be able to enter into service before 2023, quadrupling the initially estimated time frame. The affected langreans have been suffering from all kinds of problems of safety, cleanliness and discomfort, as well as impatience. In Avilés they are also waiting for progress to be made in the removal of the tracks with their underground in parallel with the ria and the creation of a train and bus terminal. This project, raised by his predecessor in the ministry, is also a pressing need, which has to be combined in parallel with the resolution of another deficiency for the Avilesinos, such as the North Round to get the heavy and dangerous traffic out of the city center. Ladies and gentlemen, a unique case, it would be said that only in the matter of railway integrations in urban plots, is that of Gijón, promoted by the government of the PP, then presided over by José María Aznar, and signed in the home of an extraordinary Spaniard, Jovellanos, whose figure, by the way, is evoked in the roof of this Chamber. It was that great agreement, signed in September 2002, by the three administrations, Renfe and FEVE, to achieve railway integration in the city with centrality and intermodality as essential references. However, and since then, as long as the PSOE has had the opportunity to put sticks on the wheels of the only serious project in the vicinity, known as metrotren, it has not wasted the opportunity. Now, however, the pandemic period has gone ahead with the consensus and the socialists, unilaterally, have broken the very broad political and social agreement reached in the city, and they want us to go back to the exit box to move away from the future station by placing it in the environment of Moreda, shredding one of the key elements of any great European station, centrality. Gijón does not happen anywhere in the world; because of the socialist decisions it has today a chamizo station, baptized as provisional, that has frightened since its inauguration in 2011 three million railway users; for thirteen years a tunnel of almost 4 km without use and flooded; and now, contrary to what is done throughout Europe, they want to distance the future terminal station from the urban center. In addition to paralysing, with the most varied pretexts, the commissioning of metrotrain and its tunnel, built to bring the railway closer to the citizens with several underground stations, to capture travelers, to vertebrate the territory, to improve the mobility, to solve the disaster of the vicinity by uniting the centres of Oviedo and Gijón, the university campuses, and to remove the surface traffic of the most polluted area of the region; the PSOE also stole to Asturias, in 2005, the high speed, with 4000 million euros invested and misdirected. Also, following the colossal step behind the inauguration and commissioning in 2011, by the former socialist government, of the station in the background of the sack of Leon, which penalizes the travel time between Asturias and Madrid within the north-northwest corridor, his government has decided to apply in the variant of Pajares the Iberian width, which disconnects Asturias from the European railway directives both for the transport of passengers and for the transport of goods. Asturians are not second-rate citizens, Mr Ábalos. We want the same deal as the rest of the Spaniards. We want high speed Spanish, not low speed Spanish. We want to equate the Madrid-Asturias line with the rest of the Spanish AVE lines and not become a railway ghetto. Port links are also not free from oblivion and neglect. There is not a single railway reason or constitutional fairness for the Madrid-Asturias high-speed line not to be equal to the other AVE lines of the rest of Spain, and the connections of El Musel and Avilés are not European wide; as the pretexts of ADIF are not fit to renounce now one of the objectives for which the great and expensive works to drill Guadarrama and Pajares, which was to communicate Madrid and Oviedo in less than two and a half hours. All sections of the platform are completed, the rest of the actions, tracks, electrification, signalling systems, are in construction or with advanced contracting. A definitive solution has been given to the technical criteria-based width scheme. The necessary passenger and freight traffic capacity will be guaranteed by the new tunnels of the variant. The development of the works will allow for the start of the tests in 2021. And as a demonstration of the momentum we are giving, just two weeks ago we awarded the contract for the execution of the external civil works in the variant of Pajares, with a budget of more than 10 million euros. For the last stage, Pola de Lena-Asturian cities, it will be the indicative strategy for the development, renovation and maintenance of the railway network of general interest over the next few years, which will establish the criteria on which to plan and design the finalization of the large high-speed corridors in Spain, including the connection with these Asturian cities. The completion of the high-speed operation and the improvement of the existing network to extend its benefits will continue to be a key pillar of investment in railway infrastructure. In short, bringing high-speed services to a given city must be carried out according to rationality, efficiency and sustainability criteria, especially as regards infrastructure, and in each case must be adapted to the constraints and particularities of each corridor. [[The Lord]] Our railway priorities for the main cities of Asturias - Oviedo, Gijón and Avilés - are consistent with the new model of mobility and are aligned with those identified in the Nearness Plan of Asturias. With these premises we are influencing the actions with the highest added value in the connections between Oviedo, Gijón and Avilés, with the aim of improving the capacity of the infrastructure and being able to establish new railway services. Thus we are making progress in the exploitation of the Olloniego section and the railway node of Villabona, for which in September last year we started an informative study. The second point of its interpellation is the standard wide connections of the large ports of El Musel and Avilés. The configuration designed with the expected arrival of the standard width up to Campomanes, shows the commitment to advance in the unification of the standard width throughout our network, but starting from rational planning, because to achieve this final objective it is necessary to provide continuity in both widths to our extensive and capillary railway network. Also, the networks managed by the ports of Avilés and Gijón include the facilities located in the interior; both ports have a network in Iberian width and in metric width. At the time of the availability of a timetable for putting into service the standard width in the corridor of goods that connects with the ports of Gijón and Avilés, including access to these ports, the port authorities would be able to program the standard width endowment on their networks. In short, the decision proposed by the technicians has been taken, which avoids leaving the ports of Asturias isolated, which has no impact on travel times - on the contrary, cuts the trip back a few minutes - which allows European funding and does not mortgage the future. When the general configuration of the network in Spain advises to change the width to benefit the industry and the Asturian shippers, it will change because it is naughty multipurpose. The third of the points on which you are interested is the Plan de Cercanías, which, as you say, was adopted by consensus between the Government of the Principality and the Government of Spain. In particular, ADIF has actions of more than EUR 400 million. Within them we can highlight, in the network of metric width, the renovations of the track, the improvement of accessibility in the station of Pola de Sero, replacements of steps at level in the line Fuso-Collanzo, the reinforcement of bridges, trenches in the line Gijón-Pravia, improvement of waterproofing of tunnels, removal of trees and vegetation to improve visibility throughout the network and others related to the security installations. On a conventional network, the project of the renovation of the centralized traffic control centres in León and Oviedo, whose work is already under way, should be highlighted. In addition, platform improvements, waterproofing of tunnels, improvement of the track level in localized sections and improvement of security facilities, among others, are being promoted. Renfe, in addition to having already executed about 20 million euros in actions of improvement of stations and modernization of the fleet, regarding the plan of renovation of rolling stock, in a few weeks, of course before August, will award the contest of the trains of metric width to renew the material of the lines of the former FEVE in Asturias. The fourth and final point of its inquiry is that of the underground and intermodal stations for railway integrations. The written informational study proposes two alternatives, the Moreda solution and the Railway Museum solution. We understand that this is a project for the future and an opportunity not only for Gijón, but for Asturias, to have a central enclave in which the railway will make an important contribution to making cities and improving urban and inter-urban mobility. You have no doubt that the best solution will be taken according to the risks, the functionality, the cost, the environment and the deadline. The projects that ADIF has already written are the following: the Plaza Europa station, Bibio and Viesques stations and extension to Cabueñes. ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u159	Next, urgent inquiry by the Joint Parliamentary Group on the policies to be developed by the Government of Spain to promote the connectivity and recovery of tourism in the Canary Islands. For your defence, Mr Quevedo Iturbe has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u160	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, good morning to all and everyone. Madam Minister of Tourism, I am aware that what I am going to put to you in this interpretation does not really affect exclusively the Ministry of Tourism, but we also believe that in this second stage of the pandemic, which is social and economic, the Ministry of Tourism must assume a clear leadership role. Because the burden of tourism in the Spanish State is 12% on GDP and about the generation of employment depends on tourism, but in the Canary Islands it turns out that the impact on GDP is 35% and 40% on employment, more than three times the average in the Spanish State. That is why we have been saying that there are two pandemics for the Canary Islands, although we are completely in solidarity with the rest of the state, as you can understand. The health pandemic has taken us well, I think things have been done well, and the Canary Islands is practically a virus-free territory. But the second part of the pandemic, which is social and economic, is going to impact in a devastating way in the Canary Islands after the tourist zero that we are suffering. Because the impact of tourism on the Canary Islands, if it is at zero, is simply devastating, to such an extent that, at the moment, among people who are at ERTE, that is, who are unemployed and who need help, we have more than 40% of the working population at risk. This is a monstrosity and I imagine that we understand each other on this subject without needing to give him more explanations. Look, the Canarian society, not the Government of the Canaries or New Canaries, canarian society has approved a plan for social and economic recovery by overwhelming majority, in which between its strategic priorities 4 and 5 we talk about the recovery of employment and the impulse to economic activity. And, in both cases, the reference necessarily passes through the effective reactivation of tourism on the islands for both issues. However, the Canary Islands cannot do it alone; however well things are done, what is clear is that the dependence on the tourist phenomenon in the Canary Islands is supracanary; that is, as well as we do it, it does not depend only on the Canary Islands doing it very well to recover the tourist activity in the archipelago. I will give you some information: the Canary airports are connected to 150 airports in 27 countries of the world; therefore, they manage approximately 20 million places of people who arrive in the Canary Islands each year, this is a monstrous figure that, let's do what we do, is not enough if you don't help us from the Government of Spain. Secondly, without international flights it is not possible to recover the activity; then we will explain the role that each of the sectors issuing tourism to the Canary Islands has. But, look, of 15.1 million tourists - this is said soon, eh! - in 2019, more than 13 million is foreign tourism; therefore, I think it is very clear what weighs each thing. We therefore propose in this question two types of measures, as you will have seen: a type of measures which are announced by the President of the Government and the spokesman minister, with which we agree and with which we hope that they will be implemented, logically, which are intended to protect the sector until normal. Here we have the continuity of the ERTE until a relatively normal regime is recovered, this is essential: the extension of the ERTE for tourism and associated activities; the facilitation of liquidity to tourism companies through the ICO lines, and the specific plans to boost tourism for Balearics and the Canary Islands, both archipelagos, although we have some differences. This is a set of measures that are not only dependent on you, of course, but are completely decisive. There are other measures that have to do with the connectivity and security of communications with the Canary Islands. With regard to facilitating travel to the Canary Islands, look, once the opening of Spain's borders has been decided, the Government is carrying out a series of negotiations with different countries at the European level, especially those with, say, a similar situation from the point of view of the positive evolution of the pandemic caused by COVID-19. Canary Islands must participate in the creation of safe corridors between countries with similar and positive epidemiological situations. But, although this seems obvious, we could take this for granted, we are extremely concerned about the development of the European Commission's proposals on this issue, because we need to know how the negotiations and agreements that this Spanish Government is carrying out for the establishment of corridors between different European regions, among which must of course be Canary Islands. We have doubts as to whether that is being done. On the other hand, it is necessary to ensure safety for our visitors, for workers and for residents. As far as safety is concerned, I have already said this: the Canary Islands is in practice a territory free of viruses and this is an enormously important strength that must be used. It is necessary that the reopening of the borders and of the tourist activity is carried out in sufficient security, because it is absolutely necessary that we sell the Canary Islands as a safe and responsible destination. We must ensure that all people arriving in the Canary Islands must be subjected to effective diagnostic tests in advance in order to ensure that all those who travel do not or will not be infected, because if this does not happen, we know that something like what happened with the flight to Lanzarote, which fortunately was nothing, happens to us on two flights and sinks the season. Therefore, this is an extremely serious issue. The Government of the Canary Islands has already committed itself to carrying out the tests at destination in the Canary Islands. Why? Because if we do not guarantee safety with PCR tests we will hardly convince anyone that it is worth a trip to the Canary Islands, when we are precisely in a time, as it is the summer, which is not the best season for the Canary Islands, as you know. We therefore need the Spanish Government to launch a strong international communication campaign by putting security as an essential argument. We believe that this measure needs your help; this cannot fall only into the hands of tourists themselves or tour operators because it will then be unworkable. The next issue is to encourage air connectivity with the islands. Look, we depend - I have already explained it to you - basically on international tourism; we, in a hundred percent, because the weight of Canarian tourism - the canaries will do everything they can, like the rest of the Spanish citizens, to help - is 1% and in the case of Spanish tourism it is more or less 10%, and so and everything will have to be taken stimulus measures with Spanish tourism to convince the people of the state that it is worth getting to Canaries. But we depend absolutely on external connectivity; the weight of international tourism is decisive and, moreover, summer is a low time for the tourist activity of the Canary Islands because Mediterranean destinations are opened up, they are closer, it is cheaper for airlines, many of them in bankruptcy practically, as you know, and therefore there is a tremendous risk that we will not recover a reasonable activity. For this reason - my mother, with the short time I have left! - it is necessary to promote the demand for air connections that facilitate international air connectivity with the Canary Islands and for this reason we need to adopt extraordinary and specific measures to promote connectivity. Two key packages: one, the reduction of air charges and bonuses for additional positions scheduled from July onwards. This first issue of air fares reduction was already addressed in the past crisis and led to a substantial increase, of an additional 1.6 million tourists, between 2009 and 2012. The goodness of this measure is evident. In 2020, if we reach 5 million tourists - 65 % less, with a fall in GDP of 20 % and a loss of turnover of more than EUR 9 billion - and we cannot improve these numbers, we are going to put ourselves at a very high unemployment rate, as I remind you that the previous crisis was less than this and we reached 34,7 %. This figure is monstrous. I don't know if I'm moving clearly what the problem is. Therefore, this is the first consideration. The second is that of the bonuses, and there are two measures in this area: one, the bonuses to airport charges, which has already been made with the Thomas Cook crisis with good results, but now we need to extend this idea to the new seats scheduled from July to the Canary Islands, both on national, state and international flights. On the other hand, the reduction in the rate of en route air navigation, the so-called route rate that Enaire manages, as you know. What we want is something very simple: as this penalizes distance, apply the same rate to us as to that place of the Spanish State that is closer to the Canary Islands and then we will be likened to the peninsular territory, which is a completely reasonable measure. I will tell you that all these measures are neither exotic nor occurrences, they are in the Economic and Fiscal System of the Canary Islands, Article 4.4. Finally, there is an urgent need to promote a specific and temporary package of airline incentives. This is a matter that the European Commission, as you know very well, has to do with. This is a question that is related to the generation of state aid to combat the effects of COVID-19 and in which we must use - we suggest, we ask you to use with the corresponding energy - the ultraperipheral condition of the Canary Islands, because that precisely puts us in good condition for Europe to understand the additional effort to be made with the Canary Islands. I trust it. Of course, as you can imagine, we will make a very concrete motion on this, but I trust the sensitivity of the Government of Spain and that of its ministry and I hope you will understand that the Canary Islands is playing it as no one, like any other community in the State, in this post-pandemic situation. Thank you, Mr. President.

ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u162	Thank you very much, Mr President. Mr Quevedo, as you have just said, the Canary Islands is facing a very complex situation as a result of the impact of the health crisis. As you said, the Canary Islands has a uniqueness for obvious reasons related to its geographical location and its high dependence on tourism, which, as you said, is around 35% of its GDP and 40% of its employment. These two circumstances, an ultraperipheral region and a high dependence on tourism, have caused the Canary Islands to be affected from the outset by the restrictions on mobility adopted to control the health crisis, restrictions which, as you know, have occurred in all the countries in our environment. As I said, we are very aware of the specific situation in which the Canary Islands is located, and therefore we have acted decisively from the outset so that the crisis does not jeopardize the bankruptcy of companies and to protect workers in the tourism sector, an extraordinary action, Your Honor, which has been fundamental to sustaining tourism, because the recovery has not begun now; it began with the first measures we took at the time when the pandemic was declared, measures which, as I said, have dampened the impact of this crisis and served as a containment dam to start the economic recovery as soon as possible. In this regard, let me remind you that the first measure that this ministry launched was precisely for the tourism sector. As you know, we expanded the ICO line we created last autumn after Thomas Cook's bankruptcy to 400 million euros. Also then our reaction was immediate and effective by recovering much of the air traffic after Thomas Cook's bankruptcy. This line, Your Honor, has made it possible to finance more than 200 operations in the Canary Islands worth 23 million euros. But undoubtedly the most important measure to protect the productive fabric in this extraordinary situation has been the line that we subsequently adopted, up to EUR 100 billion in public endorsements of the ICO, which, as you know, is being deployed in several sections. According to the data available until 1 June, the ICO has provided financing to more than 17 thousand companies in the Canary Islands, which have received more than EUR 25 billion in state-backed financing, accounting for 4% of the total. In fact, tourism, as I said, which is the sector most affected by the pandemic, is also the sector that has benefited most from the funding line provided by the ICO, approximately 17% of the total. We have also adopted other measures to protect the employment and bankruptcy of businesses, such as the flexibility of ERTs or the extraordinary benefit for self-employed people, which have benefited the tourism sector in a significant way, where more than 70% of the workers affiliated throughout the country are covered by one of these measures. Furthermore, within the framework of the social dialogue, as you know, we have agreed to extend the use of ERTs and to establish a procedure for the possible extension of this extraordinary mechanism, which undoubtedly involves a significant deployment of State resources to protect employment. The aim is to adapt this extraordinary and flexible system of support for ERTs in the most affected sectors and, of course, tourism will be one of them. We are also analysing with the social partners how to adapt the ERTE to accompany the process of descalation and at the same time encourage economic recovery. He will share with me that the descalation has been and is being gradual, asymmetrical and coordinated, but above all it is being a safe descalation, because we are not in a race to see who will arrive first, but to see who is safer. The biggest guarantee - I said the other day - to reactivate tourism is to keep the virus at bay. After the collective effort we have made, we must avoid going back to the exit box, which would certainly be fatal to the credibility and image of Spain. You will agree with me, Mr Quevedo, on the crucial importance of restoring the strength and confidence of the Spanish tourism sector, of recovering economic activity in a framework in which we can guarantee the safety and health of all people who come to our wonderful destinations. From the ministry that I run we have commissioned the Institute for the Spanish Tourist Quality to draw up more than twenty guides and protocols that will undoubtedly be fundamental in order to be able to restart the activity with the maximum health guarantees, but also to offer confidence to the visitors, to the workers and, as I said, to the residents of the destinations. They are guides that are already available and validated by the Ministry of Health. As I said, they have been developed in concert with the autonomous communities and social partners and have very clear specifications on service, cleaning and disinfection, maintenance and risk management for the different subsectors of the tourism sector. In addition, we are already in a process of certification of these guides, both through ONE and ISO, and we are also developing badges to make our destination safe. The first one you pointed out throughout your speech: health security. As I said, security is already well advanced through these protocols, which will also be guides of good practice for many countries in our environment, since Spain has participated in these months in the crisis committee of the World Tourism Organization. Last week we had the opportunity to present these guides, so Spain will undoubtedly be a benchmark of good practices in the field of health security, because for us the priority is to regain confidence as a safe destination. Another area of this support plan concerns the strengthening of the economic measures that we have already implemented and that require continuity. We will strengthen financing and liquidity in the tourism sector with a new sub-tram in the specific ICO line of endorsements for tourism with EUR 25 billion. Also, as you know, the tripartite monitoring committee which is currently working on the definition of the accompanying measures in the field of employment that will be necessary after 30 June, when it completes - as you know - the extension of ERTE, is working to make this measure more flexible, so that tourist activities can have an extension of this important measure to protect employment and also to protect businesses, allowing, inter alia, the gradual reincorporation of workers according to the level of recovery of activity, as you pointed out, and establishing incentives for the training and retraining of workers in sectors where activity is not fully recovered. A plan that we will also strengthen from the point of view of investment in digitisation and sustainable environments in both pioneering tourist destinations and inland destinations. A plan in which we will also strengthen the network of intelligent tourist destinations with 75 million euros, a network in which, as you know, several canary destinations participate. Among other measures, the plan also includes the improvement of tourist knowledge. In these months of the pandemic crisis we have needed and developed a whole system of tourist information that will allow us to make better decisions to develop the right tourist policies. More real-time indicators, data and information that we will share with the autonomous communities and the tourism sector as a whole. Finally, you were talking about the promotion, the promotion of Spain as a safe and sustainable destination not only for national tourism, but also for international tourism. With regard to the coordination in Europe to which he referred earlier and which is undoubtedly a fundamental aspect for the Canary Islands, but also for the countries within their issuing markets, I must say that on 11 May nine countries led by Spain presented a joint statement at the last informal meeting of Ministers of Tourism to ask the European Union for a recovery plan aimed at alleviating the negative impact of the pandemic on the European tourism sector, a plan in which we specifically call for attention to the problems of the island and outermost regions such as the Canary Islands. ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u168	An urgent appeal from the Basque Parliamentary Group to the Minister of Industry, Trade and Tourism on the measures envisaged to give flexibility to the key sectors of economic activity. [[EAJ-PNV]] For your defence, Mrs Sagastizabal has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u169	Thank you, President. Good morning, minister. This is the third interpellation in relation to industry. In three weeks, after interpellations from different political parties. And it is true, even though we are not ideologically linked to them, it seems that what does exist is a concern for industry. My intention with this interpellation is to convey to you our concern, to make a constructive criticism, but also to re-express the readiness for agreement and dialogue. You often like to talk about certainties and we raise one of those certainties as a starting point for this health crisis, which is also uncertain. It is clear that the crisis is asymmetric and therefore its exit as well. The structure of the productive fabric is very different within the composite state in which we are, and is the productive structure of a region one of the main factors to explain its resilience. Thus, those more diversified economies, with more industry, are always in a better starting position: in the face of a crisis the fall is smaller and the resistance is greater. That is why it is necessary to bet on industry. The Basque Nationalist Party has always done so, we have been betting and betting on industry as an engine of the economy. We have always shown concern about the absence of an industrial policy regardless of who has been ruling, a concern that the sector itself highlights, and also some of the rapporteurs in the economic revival working group of the reconstruction pact. I could say that industrial policy is exclusive competence, and indeed it is, but it will also agree with me that it must be coordinated with the various ministries and with its own - ministries which, at best, are not exclusive, such as those of energy or work - so that decisions are coordinated and do not undermine the viability and competence of the industrial sector. In his last appearance before the COVID I told him that in recent times in the industry patches were being put out or, rather, extinguishing fires to cover the problems in the face of critical situations that were coming, such as Navantia, ArcelorMittal, Alcoa, and those other ones derived from somewhat hasty ads, such as diesel, which were evidenced by a lack of coordination between some ministries and whose consequences, for example, in the automotive sector resulted in a decrease in sales. With this we certainly do not want to attribute to your Government the responsibility for industrial crises, because it is clear that an industrial crisis does not arise from one day to the next, but is the result of many factors and decisions, mainly by companies over time. But the government can do something, it can lay the foundations and policies to attract and retain businesses and thus reduce all these risks. But now we are in a different context, with a significant fall in activity, a situation still of uncertainty, with an economic impact of which we have no precedent or sufficient information. The expansion of the virus and its effect on productive activity are putting sectors with a high impact on the economy in check by dragging even the auxiliary industry. Therefore, in addition to specific and general measures valid for all companies regardless of the sector to which they belong - which we do not doubt - those sectors that are key in the economy need a specific boost. I am referring to automotive, aeronautics and machine tools, sectors of high impact on employment, technological development and the development of related sectors, which are also highly innovative and highly exporting. This is, in short, the motivation for the interpellation we are making. Again, minister, although we understand that the management of COVID is complex, we have the feeling that its ministry is absent in those strategic and most relevant decisions made during the crisis that affected and affect these sectors in full. I am referring to the closure of the activity at twelve o'clock on a Sunday night when his ministry knew perfectly well that the industry needs a scheduled and orderly shutdown to minimize the damage to the production chain. It also fails to take specific measures for these sectors. You can tell me at best that without health there is no economy, but that without economy there is no health. It is not about that dilemma, but about reconciling economics and health, reconciling economics and safety at work, as they are pillars that stand together. Two weeks ago I was asking him about the car measures and he practically didn't say anything to me and then we did find ourselves in the press a car plan that we barely know anything about today. Well, we know you're late. A plan that we understand will depend on the European funds, because you said last week that you inherited a rickshaw budget, that of 2018, and while it is true that this budget after two extensions does not give more of itself, it must also be said that it is up to the current government to look for the necessary arrangements for a new budget. Now they have that opportunity, they have to take those first steps - we are at the door of July - and that is where we will see the priorities and figures that mark their industrial policy. Of course, I do not doubt his goodwill or his prodialogue attitude, but it is not enough. We are at a time when it requires initiatives, concrete and clear measures, but also risky. One cannot go on with a passive vision of industrial policy, which has only been done to encourage de-industrialisation. Speaking of certainties, not investing in industrial policy is expensive and hence the need now not to make the same mistakes. The past serves us to learn what we must do and what we must not do and to look above all at the future. I am going to focus on three sectors and the effect of the crises on them, but I believe that it will also be extrapolable to other sectors affected by this crisis and that they will also need the same reflection and ministry to attend to them. With regard to the machine tool, the long periods of duration of its products enabled its workload. However, new orders, technical assistance services, the reception of factory machinery, the inability to travel, to meet customers and to meet them, all of which are essential activities, have stopped almost all the demand. Moreover, this is going to have a much bigger reflection from September onwards. The aviation sector is one of the worst off due to the drastic reduction in overall air mobility, with a 90 % drop in passenger traffic. The manufacturing cadences of the different models of the main customers of the companies of the sector have sunk, especially because of the cancellation and lack of new orders of the airlines and, moreover, because of the great uncertainty future. Automotive. It represents 10% of GDP. The data are eloquent: their production has fallen by 97.5 for tourism and 98 % for export. The COVID has resulted in the breakdown of its own supply model, based on a supply chain of just-in-time parts: factory stoppage, car stoppage as a result of the collapse of demand due to confinement, restrictions on mobility and closure of sales dealers. In addition, it has dragged the component and auxiliary industry. It is also a sector that was immersed in the transformation of its business model: change of product towards electrification, obligation to meet emission reduction targets, uncertainty in regulation, and with a low sales market. These sectors, Minister, are addressing this situation with the implementation of adjustment measures to adapt to the new reality of the market, reducing their operating costs and their productive and organizational capacity, maintaining employment and re-adjusting the partial organization of their work so as not to lose the talent of the people. But these measures alone are not enough, they need more flexibility to adapt, because uncertainty is about to stay for a while and they need a safer and more predictable environment for new investments, avoiding bottlenecks and obstacles. We all know that the productive sectors make their long-term decisions, and this again requires certainty and legal certainty. And beyond short-term shock measures, a coordinated plan is also needed to support industry, to reactivate investment, an investment that also generates economic and social return, and for industry to be able to face with financial muscle not only the economic consequences of COVID, but also the new challenges it is going to face. Spain, once again, is going to lag behind in giving an industrial response in this new context. While the competitors of Europe, France, Germany, and the rest of the countries already have specific support, plans to maintain and strengthen the most competitive industry, here these sectors, in the absence of specific government news, are defending themselves and adapting to the new market circumstances. Two certainties, Minister: while France has an automotive plan, here the announced one is delayed; while yesterday France announced a plan for the aeronautical sector, here, for the moment, does not even arise. You also tell me something more about that announced automotive plan today, but I'm sure you'll be going back to talking about the ICO lines, the ERTEs; horizontal, general and valid measures for all sectors, without taking into account their specificities. Obviously, they are positive measures. We have supported with our vote all the royal decrees regulating these measures, but I also say to you that, with regard to ERTE, you have to talk and coordinate with the Minister of Labour. They extend ERTE sectorally by force majeure until the end of the year and facilitate an agile and flexible transition to new ERTE for economic and organizational reasons. It is a decision that must be made now. We are not asking you generalised ERTE, we are talking about ERTE for sectors that are competitive, because they have product and market, because they are viable sectors, with growth potential and that generate value through innovation, but they still need and for a longer period of time more flexibility to accompany the return of business activity and to be able to reintegrate the entire workforce as there is demand. It is because of something that ERTE is characterized by flexicurity: to facilitate the flexibility of the company to adapt to new situations, especially in crisis situations, and safety for workers. There is a need for a shared strategy based on the assets available. We must build on the existing and give them a new impetus to sustain the activity and employment of the sector in the short term. But future competitiveness must also be improved. It is necessary to defend the existing industry, to ensure its sustainability, to build - as I said - on existing capacities and, in turn, to develop a new industry. Industry is a key sector to get out of this crisis and a good shock absorber for the future. ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u177	We continue with the point corresponding to the validation or repeal of royal decree-laws. First, there is the debate on the validation or repeal of Royal Decree-Law 19/2020 of 26 May, which adopts complementary measures in the fields of agriculture, science, economics, employment and social security, and taxation to alleviate the effects of COVID-19. To present the royal decree-law, the Minister of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u178	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear on behalf of the Government to ask for the validation of Royal Decree-Law 19/2020, which provides for the extension of certain extraordinary measures in the field of agricultural employment which, as you will recall, we adopted as a result of the declaration of the state of alarm a few weeks ago, as well as of other social and labour measures which have been shown to be essential in order to cope with the times at which we are faced, and particularly the health crisis and its consequences, from the point of view of health, but also from the economic and social point of view. This is a choral work, this is a royal decree-law that involves nine ministries, which has up to twenty-two main themes in its content - I will stop only in the most significant ones -. It covers the agricultural areas, as I have indicated, but also scientific, economic, employment and social security and taxation, all of them to promote the recovery of our economy, economic recovery and social recovery, and to ensure that all the rules that we have adopted in the Government since the declaration of the state of alarm are properly complemented and allow us to achieve, in this new phase where we enter and in this new period, the best collective results for the whole of Spanish society, for all Spaniards and, ultimately, for Spain. I shall start with measures in the agricultural field, also in fisheries and related matters. As you will recall, the Royal Decree-Law 13/2020, which was a genuine innovation in agricultural employment, established extraordinary measures of flexibility to maintain agricultural activity and, in particular, the recruitment of temporary workers until 30 June this year, all to ensure all the necessary agricultural activities, those relating to the basically fruit and vegetables sector, and all those ordinary maintenance and activities in the agricultural and livestock sector. I would say that the objective of this royal decree-law has been achieved, to the extent that not a single agrarian task has remained to be done because of the lack of workers willing to do so. I believe that this is an economic and social achievement, which is why I am bringing this extension to your ladies and gentlemen until 30 September, in order to give greater peace and security to the whole of the agricultural sector. As stated earlier, this Royal Decree-Law 13/2020, as a complement to ordinary recruitment and also to the facilitation of the passage to the different stages and the rules on the control of external borders, has allowed us to return certain workers - for example, permanent discontinuous workers who were in previous campaigns present in the sector - to carry them out from the end of June. I therefore believe that the objective has been achieved, and I thank your ladies and gentlemen for the majority support they gave at the time to the royal decree-law, the extension of which we are bringing today. A second aspect is that of one of the social consequences, I would say that small in number, but very significant from the point of view of involvement, which refers to a migration issue, the granting of a residence and work permit to those young foreigners between eighteen and twenty-one years old who have actually received and worked, under this royal decree-law, and to whom the Government considers that they should be given at least one residence and a two-year work permit, which can be extended by two more years. A third point that is very specific, but important, is the amendment of the State Maritime Fisheries Act to allow the shellfish sector and, in particular, the majority of its players, who are women - shellfish - to benefit from the European aid that we have obtained when the European Maritime and Fisheries Fund becomes more flexible. That's why we bring that change. Finally, a point of social security, but linked to the agricultural sector, which is the amendment of the General Social Security Act. As you know, a self-employed worker under the Special Agricultural Scheme may have up to two workers. In some cases there were holdings of two holders, and in order to allow a third worker to be employed and to maintain his status as self-employed in the Special Agricultural Scheme, we bring this amendment before your ladies and gentlemen. The second specific area of this royal decree-law is the scientist. The European Union is carrying out an ambitious programme to develop fusion as an energy source, in an unprecedented international partnership that affects a number of countries, China, Korea, India, Japan, Russia and others. In this context Spain submitted in August 2017 a candidacy under the Ifmif-Dones Programme to carry out a project to carry out these tasks. The decree contains the authorization of the creation of a consortium between the Government of Spain and the Junta de Andalucía so that such a project, whose candidacy was presented - as I said - in August 2017, can be carried out, task of the Ministry of Science and Innovation. In the economic sphere, there are a number of measures to which I would like to refer. Firstly, in the field of telecommunications, as you will recall, ladies and gentlemen, on the occasion of the decree of the state of alarm - and in order to be able to concentrate on the essential task of managing communications - portability and the possibility of keeping mobile phone numbers during this period were suspended. Well, in this royal decree-law we lift that suspension, so that we can proceed again to something that I believe the citizens as a whole appreciate very much. As well as a second aspect, because of the economic and social implications of the current situation, i.e. the possibility of splitting and postponing the debt that individual subscribers may have, from the moment of the state of alarm until 30 June, in connection with their expenses in terms of telephone and data. Secondly, on the economic front. The recommendation of the European Central Bank, the banking supervisor, that credit institutions refrain from distributing dividends and from making irrevocable commitments to distribute them for the years 2019 and 2020, has a practical consequence in relation to bank foundations and, logically, to dividends, which are their source of financing. Therefore, following what the European Central Bank indicated, we have decided to suspend the obligation to provide the Reserve Fund this year in such a way that the legislation can be complied with in this extraordinary circumstance in which we find ourselves. Another very important measure in this economic area is the European temporary support instrument to mitigate the risks of unemployment in an emergency, the so-called SURE instrument, which, as you know, has been able to provide up to EUR 100 billion in the Eurogroup area. It is very important because it is clearly a mechanism to support the financing of ERTs in terms of unemployment coverage. Of these 100 billion, 25 billion are supported by the Member States in relation to the share of their gross national product. Spain is responsible for a figure slightly higher than the EUR 2 billion to be endorsed, and in this royal decree-law the Minister of Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation is authorized to subscribe to the corresponding endorsement. Likewise, in terms of guarantees, you know that with the National State Greetings Programme - for the amount of EUR 100 billion we have given as a liquidity response in this situation - budgetary coverage is given through this royal decree-law to this measure adopted. Finally, in this economic area, a final measure refers to what is called the non-mortgage moratorium, regulated by Royal Decree-Law 11/2020 of 31 March. Article 21 of this Royal Decree-Law is amended so that financial leases can also be included within its scope. The fourth area of application is labour and social security standards. The Royal Decree-Law 18/2020 is amended to incorporate references to the Social Institute of the Navy and to facilitate the implementation of ERTs in this area. There is a measure on which I would like to stop for a moment, because it seems particularly significant to me that it is the recognition as an accident at work of social security benefits that causes staff serving in health and social health centres, in the exercise of their profession, who have contracted the disease - on the occasion and, of course, the cause of COVID-19 - during any phase of this pandemic, because they have been professionally exposed to this risk. We have made an express recognition, I believe, of the work of the front-line health workers, which, from the point of view of work and social security, is a minimum recognition of their work that we must mean. Finally, the fifth area is the tax and budgetary area. In this area, of the measures included in the Royal Decree-Law, I have indicated to your ladies and gentlemen only those which have a more relevant significance. The first concerns budgetary changes in the expenditure budget of the Ministry of Inclusion, Social Security and Migration, which provide social security for the financing of non-contributory benefits, and also to balance the impact on social security accounts resulting from the COVID-19 pandemic. Secondly, the extension to four months of the repayment period of interest on the postponements of article 14 of Royal Decree-Law 7/2020. Thirdly, to facilitate the declaration of corporation tax by extending to 15 November the deadline by which the ordinary general meeting of the company concerned may take place. And also in this area, and exceptionally for the year 2020 and until 30 September, the period of time in which the list of fraudsters will be made public to the Public Treasury. Finally, in this area, I would like to refer to the acceptance of the amendment of the current rule on the expenditure limit on public remuneration, in order to make possible the application, for this year, by 2020, of the agreement signed between the Ministry of the Interior, the trade unions of the National Police and the associations of the Civil Guard on agreements on tax equalization. As you see, ladies and gentlemen - and I have made a brief summary - these are a set of measures which all of them are complementary to those already adopted by this Government, which are in the sense of seeking the earliest recovery of our economy and of our society, and obviously in the service of Spain and of all the Spaniards, which is why I ask you to vote in favour of the validation of this royal decree-law. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u180	Thank you very much, Madam President. Good morning, Minister for Agriculture, Fisheries and Food. It is a circumstance that does not cease to produce distrust in a norm that consists of twelve articles, since it seems that what is sought is to predispose the reader about the goodness of what is to come next, reminding us of the famous Latin expression: excusatio non petita, accusatio manifesto. But it is not only the quantity, but also the quality. If we go to the text of the explanatory statement itself, we see that it tends to repeat the content of the article by praising its goodness and those of the Government, without respecting what must be the true essence of every preamble, which is precisely to explain the reasons that have led the delegated legislator to decide to implement a series of administrative changes due to a previous factual situation that advises his intervention. It is therefore relevant to know what the statement of reasons for interpreting the norm in the light of its spirit and purpose says, and it cannot serve other purposes such as those that appear to be pursued by the Government, which are no more than merely propaganda. This new style of the legislator, which already generates packaging, is being a constant in the Government's actions throughout this XIV Legislature, as I have already had occasion to point out in this rostrum in previous validations, and I have not been the only one who has done so. If it continues its current progress, it is not impossible to rule out that in this legislature the explanatory statements end at the end of the normative text rather than at the beginning and change their denomination to that of morale, which would be appropriate given the legislative drift of the Government. Ladies and gentlemen, going back to the wording of this royal decree-law, and despite the fact that the explanatory statement is also intended to justify the extraordinary and urgent need, it is true that there are issues referred to in the rule in which it can be considered that such circumstances do not clearly compete, such as those relating to fishing sanctions in view of the size of the molluscs or the application for an application for the construction of a demonstration plant for electric fusion energy. Neither of these two issues has any bearing on COVID-19, nor does the urgent need for the adoption of these standards seem to be appreciated. With regard to the specific measures proposed, Article 3 provides for a division and deferral of debts in electronic communications services, which does not provide for any requirement with respect to the economic conditions of the applicant. It would have been appropriate because an unnecessary postponement and/or general fragmentation may endanger the financial stability of some of the operators in the sector. It should also be noted that in a text called to become law, certain aspects are developed in detail and in concrete form that should be dealt with on a regulatory basis, such as articles 6, 7 and 8, which refer to moratoriums on financing operations, which shows a poor legislative technique. On the basis of this conclusion, it is also necessary to ask why the Government continues to turn its back on the Council of State in this new legislative product, when its necessary participation is known, in addition to its relevant assistance in trying to improve the rules. Finally, I would like to refer to article 9, entitled: Consideration as occupational contingency arising from an accident at work of diseases suffered by staff serving in health or social health facilities as a result of the SARS-CoV2 virus infection during the state of alarm. In the Government's desire to issue news reports in front of the gallery - or rather of the broadcaster - this rule is issued which may have the purpose of harming the collective to which it is supposed to benefit, and this is because the factual assumption referred to in article 9 of our current legislative framework would be described as a professional disease, as provided for in article 157 of the General Social Security Act and its development regulations. What is now being done with this royal decree-law is to introduce prejudicial requirements for the need for accreditation by occupational risk prevention and occupational health services, which, in the case of occupational disease, would not be necessary. Less understandable is the temporary validity for contagions produced up to one month after the state of alarm or for deaths after five years. What about the later ones? Are they again considered occupational disease? In conclusion, this article, either is a misconception, or is a further punishment for health professionals who have suffered so much from this crisis and who, rather than applause and toast to the sun, need their prevention work rights and conditions to be timely and effectively respected. In any case - and I conclude, Madam President - I will support the validation of this royal decree-law and its urgent processing as a bill. Thank you very much.

The temporary recruitment of workers is encouraged by the introduction of extraordinary measures to make employment more flexible in the social and labour fields. In the field of labour and social security, the consolidated text of the General Social Security Act is amended so that, in the case of holdings with more than one holder, more workers may be hired proportionally in order to be included in the special scheme for self-employed persons. The Royal Decree-Law 18/2020 of social measures in defence of employment is amended in order to incorporate the necessary references to the Social Institute of the Navy. The unemployment benefit of public performing artists is adjusted, in order to clarify that it is the extraordinary access itself to the benefit that responds to the health crisis of COVID-19, without it being appropriate for artists to believe that their concrete situation of lack of activity derives from it. This Royal Decree-Law extends coverage to staff who provide care in health or social health services. It is thus recognized that benefits to this group, when they have contracted COVID-19, are considered to be derived from an accident at work and in cases of death it is also understood that the cause is an accident at work, provided that it occurs within five years of the contagion. With regard to tax measures, the approved decree introduces the necessary adjustments for the presentation of the corporate tax declaration to the reality caused by the pandemic. It should be borne in mind that Royal Decree-Law 8/2020 extended the time limits available to companies for the preparation and subsequent approval of their annual accounts due to the health emergency. In the economic and financial sphere, the suspension of portability is abolished, and the previous fully competitive situation is restored. From this point on, it is appropriate for the telecommunications market to regain full operation and to allow the number of subscribers to be retained again in the event of a change of operator or portability of the number. A procedure is also in place that makes it easier for subscribers to satisfy outstanding invoices in a flexible manner. In the case of mortgage and non-mortgage moratoria, this Royal Decree incorporates a special regime for moratoria agreements reached between lenders and their clients. This special regime not only extends the group of persons benefiting from a debt deferral beyond those economically vulnerable, but also allows the latter to extend the deferral after the expiry of the period of the legal moratorium. ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u210	Thank you very much, Mr Vice-President of the Government. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Next, the Minister of Inclusion, Social Security and Migration has the floor on behalf of the Government to present the Royal Decree-Law.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-06-10-CD200610.u211	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. The Royal Decree-Law 20/2020 establishing the minimum living income and the public policy that is born with it is the result of three profound reflections, shared not only by this ministry and by this government, but, I believe, by society as a whole. The first and most important reflection is the recognition that our country has a structural problem of inequality and poverty, a problem that, although magnificent in times of crisis, does not return in times of good times. The European authorities, independent reports, economists and specialised sociologists remind us over and over again in the European context that our incomes are poorly redistributed because of the extraordinarily low incomes of the poorest households. As in all complex social phenomena, this anomaly has many causes, but it is clear that one of them has to do with political decisions or, rather, the absence of such decisions. We see this in the comparative figures of poverty before and after state intervention through transfers: while social transfers can significantly reduce severe poverty in most European countries, in Spain they do so in a very limited way. And that's because, unlike what happens in our environment, we don't have a strong and powerful network to protect last resort rents. The fact that almost all the responsibility for this policy has fallen to the autonomous communities, whose minimum income programmes have varied considerably in size and generosity, has in fact led to inequalities among citizens, which these policies should aim to reduce, even widen over time. After a collective effort of reflection, analysis and considerable debate, Spanish society has managed to put this problem at the centre of the political agenda. That, in a context like the present, we have made efforts, efforts and resources to improve the lives of the weakest, I think it makes us a little better and I think, ladies and gentlemen, that we should all congratulate ourselves on that. The second reflection is that the social and economic transformations of recent decades force us to rethink public policies that aim to protect the well-being and opportunities of the most vulnerable. We cannot, for example, link all social protection to employment. Work careers are now very volatile. The reality of precarious employment affects many workers and labour poverty, what the Anglo-Saxons call working poor, is a phenomenon that is unfortunately becoming more frequent. Nor can we ignore the different realities of today's vulnerable households, such as the extraordinary difficulties faced by single-parent families with limited resources; difficulties that unjustly miss the opportunities of children growing up with them. Public policies cannot live behind the back of the society in which they deploy their effects. The society of the twenty-first century demands, ladies and gentlemen, policies of the twenty-first century. Thirdly, the COVID pandemic has forced us to speed up the correction of these problems. The sudden slowdown in economic activity imposed by health measures has made the costs for poorer households even more visible by the absence of a last income protection network. Although we have implemented policies to protect the incomes of those affected by the pandemic, the most vulnerable families are systematically faced with more barriers to accessing them. Indeed, the pandemic has forced us to speed up the timelines for implementing the new policy, but we have always believed that, despite the urgency of the situation, there was no alternative to designing this policy with the rigour and analysis it deserved. The real decree-law establishing minimum living income is a complex policy, but essentially consists of two elements: on the one hand, a highly redistributive benefit designed to combat, in the most efficient way possible, the most extreme forms of poverty, but, on the other hand, it is a set of policies - yes, a set of policies - aimed at ensuring that the satisfaction of basic material needs serves to move the beneficiaries of the benefit from exclusion and the lack of opportunities towards full participation in society and the economy as a whole. The minimum living income must be understood, ladies and gentlemen, simultaneously as a tool for combating poverty and a lever for social inclusion. Although minimum vital income will inevitably be focused on helping the most vulnerable households - those with defined minimum income and assets - it should not be understood as much as a policy aimed at specific groups or individuals, but rather as a collective insurance that, on a permanent basis, rescues people at every moment who suffer severe material deficiencies and pushes them, leads them, towards integration into society. The first major innovation that introduces the design of the minimum living income is that, both in calculating the magnitude of the benefit and in designing the inclusion itineraries, it will take into account the economic, family and social reality of the beneficiaries. This is, I think, one of the most innovative elements of this policy: putting the recipients at the centre. It is public policy that adapts to the needs of collectors and not the other way around, as is often the case. That is why the structure of the household, its income and its assets will be taken into account when calculating the amount of the benefit. This is truly modern, ladies and gentlemen. The second novelty is the centrality of the inclusion objective that is vertebrate throughout the norm. For some, inclusion will involve new educational opportunities, for others, a solution to a specific health condition, but for the majority, inclusion will involve entry into the labour market or more intense and stable participation in it. To this end, the standard incorporates a wide range of measures designed to encourage the employment of beneficiaries and to avoid what the literature usually refers to as the poverty trap. But as important and novel as the content of the concrete design of the standard is how we have arrived at it. The design of minimum vital income has been based on deep, rigorous and meticulous work. We have combined all available information on the income, wealth and career paths of each of the people who make up all Spanish households. To this end, we have had the collaboration, delivery and professionalism of social security officials, the Tax Agency, the National Statistical Institute and other administrative units. We have also worked very closely with the Autonomous Communities. We have examined in detail the wealth of this information with the aim of delimiting very well the households to which we want to reach and to know well the reality of the potential beneficiaries. Thanks to this thorough examination, we discovered, among other things, the importance of considering not only income, but also the net assets of potential collectors. Before choosing the definitive design, we have simulated and compared many alternatives, evaluating their effectiveness in reducing poverty and their tax cost. All this work makes us reasonably sure that what we are asking to validate in Congress today is a good design of a public policy that will achieve its objectives at a perfectly manageable tax cost. And not just that. The rule provides for different provisions that promote greater formalization of the economy, with the positive effects that this can have on society, including those of a collection and tax nature. And, of course, in view of the messages that have slipped over the last few days, minimum vital income is incompatible with situations of administrative irregularity. We are humble and we are aware that it will be the reality that ultimately gives us or takes away our reason and that is why we like to think of the minimum income as a living policy, which will be constantly learning from the reality in which it is intended to affect and which will be evaluated continuously, before, during and after its deployment, and not only by us, but - most importantly - also by the rest of the administrations involved and independent bodies, as well as by analysts, researchers and representatives of the third sector of social action, within an approach defined by the norm itself as a 360-degree evaluation. The minimum vital income brings, as it were, already incorporated the home assessment. This will make you the policy that will improve as you progress over time, and this is also a novelty. In addition to methodological rigour and constant empirical contrast, we have designed this policy with the best spirit of dialogue that we have been able to achieve in the last few months. We have explained this and listened to the parliamentary groups, the autonomous governments, the municipalities, the social partners, employers and trade unions and, of course, the third sector. Despite the reasonable and even desirable differences, I must say that I have found in all of them - in all of you I should say - a strong spirit of commitment to the central ideas underlying this policy: the need to correct the problem of inequality and structural poverty affecting our most vulnerable population, the desire to adapt public policies to the economic and social reality of our time and the urgency of meeting the needs arising as a result of the pandemic. Thanks to this sincere dialogue and to the suggestions, concerns and ideas of each other, the norm is much better today and, moreover, it is more of all. I firmly believe that we are faced with a policy that outlines a framework for action in which, beyond sound ideological discrepancies, we should all feel relatively comfortable, a policy that respects and even enhances the capacity of the autonomous communities to meet the specific needs of their territories and that has local entities and civil society to better understand the needs of the beneficiaries. Although we sometimes suggest otherwise, public opinion deserves to know that political representatives are capable of dialogue and building and I would like this policy to be remembered as an example of this. Yesterday, in this very house, in my appearance at the Committee on Labour, Inclusion, Social Security and Migration, we had an enriching debate in an atmosphere of cordiality and respect that citizens deserve to witness more often. This spirit of constructive dialogue must always be welcomed, but for a policy like this, which is born in response to a structural deficit of our welfare State, which therefore aims to deploy its effects several legislatures and which will require in its day-to-day active involvement of various levels of government and civil society, cooperation is not only desirable but absolutely indispensable. This is why the norm also incorporates a co-governance model, in which different administrations, social actors and civil society will be central actors in policy development and evaluation. It's over, ladies and gentlemen. What they now have to vote on is a rule that corrects a historical deficit in our welfare model, that puts us at the forefront in the use of data for the development of public policies and, above all, that dignifies us as a country. I ask you not only to vote in favour of it, but to be fully involved in its deployment as of tomorrow. You can be sure that in our ministry and in the Government, with generosity and will of understanding, we will continue as always counting on you. Thank you very much.
